      commands: Record<string, Info>
    }
    
    export const Event = {
      Executed: LegacyEvent.CommandExecuted,
    }
    
    export const Info = Schema.Struct({
      name: Schema.String,
      description: Schema.optional(Schema.String),
      agent: Schema.optional(Schema.String),
      model: Schema.optional(Schema.String),
      source: Schema.optional(Schema.Literals(["command", "mcp", "skill"])),
      // Some command templates are lazy promises from MCP prompt resolution.
      template: Schema.Unknown,
      subtask: Schema.optional(Schema.Boolean),
      maxArguments: Schema.optional(Schema.Int.check(Schema.isGreaterThanOrEqualTo(0))),
      fileArgument: Schema.optional(Schema.Boolean),
      hints: Schema.Array(Schema.String),
    }).annotate({ identifier: "Command" })
    
    export type Info = Omit<Schema.Schema.Type<typeof Info>, "template"> & { template: Promise<string> | string }
    
    export function hints(template: string) {
      const result: string[] = []
      const numbered = template.match(/\$\d+/g)
      if (numbered) {
        for (const match of [...new Set(numbered)].sort()) result.push(match)
      }
      if (template.includes("$ARGUMENTS")) result.push("$ARGUMENTS")
      return result
    }
    
    export const Default = {
      INIT: "init",
      REVIEW: "review",
      ASSOCIATE: "associate",
    } as const
    
    export interface Interface {
      readonly get: (name: string) => Effect.Effect<Info | undefined>
      readonly list: () => Effect.Effect<Info[]>
    }
    
    export class Service extends Context.Service<Service, Interface>()("@opencode/Command") {}
    
    const layer = Layer.effect(
      Service,
      Effect.gen(function* () {
        const config = yield* Config.Service
        const mcp = yield* MCP.Service
        const skill = yield* Skill.Service
    
        const init = Effect.fn("Command.state")(function* (ctx: InstanceContext) {
          const cfg = yield* config.get()
          const bridge = yield* EffectBridge.make()
          const commands: Record<string, Info> = {}
    
          commands[Default.INIT] = {
            name: Default.INIT,
            description: "guided AGENTS.md setup",
            source: "command",
            get template() {
              return PROMPT_INITIALIZE.replace("${path}", ctx.worktree)
            },
            hints: hints(PROMPT_INITIALIZE),
          }
          commands[Default.REVIEW] = {
            name: Default.REVIEW,
            description: "review changes [commit|branch|pr], defaults to uncommitted",
            source: "command",
            get template() {
              return PROMPT_REVIEW.replace("${path}", ctx.worktree)
            },
            subtask: true,
            hints: hints(PROMPT_REVIEW),
          }
          commands[Default.ASSOCIATE] = {
            name: Default.ASSOCIATE,
            description: "associate functions in a file with their tests",
            source: "command",
            template: PROMPT_ASSOCIATE,
            maxArguments: 1,
            fileArgument: true,
            hints: hints(PROMPT_ASSOCIATE),
          }
    
          for (const [name, command] of Object.entries(cfg.command ?? {})) {
            commands[name] = {
              name,
              agent: command.agent,
              model: command.model,
              description: command.description,
              source: "command",
              get template() {
                return command.template
              },
              subtask: command.subtask,
              hints: hints(command.template),
            }
          }
    
          for (const [name, prompt] of Object.entries(yield* mcp.prompts())) {
            commands[name] = {
              name,
              source: "mcp",
              description: prompt.description,
              get template() {
                return bridge.promise(
                  mcp
                    .getPrompt(
                      prompt.client,
                      prompt.name,
                      prompt.arguments
                        ? Object.fromEntries(prompt.arguments.map((argument, i) => [argument.name, `$${i + 1}`]))

            command: input.command,
            agent: input.agent,
          })
          const cmd = yield* commands.get(input.command)
          if (!cmd) {
            const available = (yield* commands.list()).map((c) => c.name)
            const hint = available.length ? ` Available commands: ${available.join(", ")}` : ""
            const error = new NamedError.Unknown({ message: `Command not found: "${input.command}".${hint}` })
            yield* events.publish(Session.Event.Error, { sessionID: input.sessionID, error: error.toObject() })
            throw error
          }
          const agentName = cmd.agent ?? input.agent
    
          const raw = input.arguments.match(argsRegex) ?? []
          const args = raw.map((arg) => arg.replace(quoteTrimRegex, ""))
          if (cmd.maxArguments !== undefined && args.length > cmd.maxArguments) {
            const error = new NamedError.Unknown({
              message: `Command "/${cmd.name}" accepts at most ${cmd.maxArguments} argument${cmd.maxArguments === 1 ? "" : "s"}.`,
            })
            yield* events.publish(Session.Event.Error, { sessionID: input.sessionID, error: error.toObject() })
            throw error
          }
          if (cmd.fileArgument && args[0]) {
            const ctx = yield* InstanceState.context

              yield* events.publish(Session.Event.Error, { sessionID: input.sessionID, error: error.toObject() })
              throw error
            }
          }
          const templateCommand = yield* Effect.promise(async () => cmd.template)
    
          const placeholders = templateCommand.match(placeholderRegex) ?? []
          let last = 0
          for (const item of placeholders) {
            const value = Number(item.slice(1))
            if (value > last) last = value
          }
    
          const withArgs = templateCommand.replaceAll(placeholderRegex, (_, index) => {
            const position = Number(index)
            const argIndex = position - 1
            if (argIndex >= args.length) return ""
            if (position === last) return args.slice(argIndex).join(" ")
            return args[argIndex]
          })
          const usesArgumentsPlaceholder = templateCommand.includes("$ARGUMENTS")
          let template = withArgs.replaceAll("$ARGUMENTS", input.arguments)
    
          if (placeholders.length === 0 && !usesArgumentsPlaceholder && input.arguments.trim()) {
            template = template + "\n\n" + input.arguments
          }
    
          const shellMatches = ConfigMarkdown.shell(template)
          if (shellMatches.length > 0) {
            const cfg = yield* config.get()
            const sh = Shell.preferred(cfg.shell)
            const results = yield* Effect.promise(() =>
              Promise.all(
                shellMatches.map(async ([, cmd]) => (await Process.text([cmd], { shell: sh, nothrow: true })).text),
              ),
            )
            let index = 0
            template = template.replace(bashRegex, () => results[index++])
          }
          template = template.trim()

          process.env.SHELL = "/bin/sh"
          Shell.preferred.reset()
          return prev
        }),
        () => fx(),
        (prev) =>
          Effect.sync(() => {
            if (prev === undefined) delete process.env.SHELL
            else process.env.SHELL = prev
            Shell.preferred.reset()
          }),
      )

    // Wait for a session's runner to enter a busy state. SessionStatus is flipped
    // inside Runner.startShell's serialized transition, so cancel can't no-op once
    // we observe it.
    const waitForBusy = (sessionID: SessionID, duration: Duration.Input = "2 seconds") =>
      pollWithTimeout(
        Effect.gen(function* () {
          const status = yield* SessionStatus.Service
          const s = yield* status.get(sessionID)
          return s.type === "busy" ? (true as const) : undefined
        }),
        `session ${sessionID} never became busy`,
        duration,
      )
    
    const hasBash = Effect.sync(() => Bun.which("bash") !== null)
    
    const deferredAsPromise = <A>(deferred: Deferred.Deferred<A>): PromiseLike<A> => ({
      then: (onfulfilled, onrejected) => {
        Effect.runFork(
          Deferred.await(deferred).pipe(
            Effect.match({
              onFailure: (error) => {
                onrejected?.(error)
              },
              onSuccess: (value) => {
                onfulfilled?.(value)
              },
            }),
          ),
        )
        return deferredAsPromise(deferred) as PromiseLike<never>
      },
    })
    
    function defer<T>() {
      let resolve!: (value: T | PromiseLike<T>) => void
      const promise = new Promise<T>((done) => {
        resolve = done
      })
      return { promise, resolve }
    }
    
    const succeedVoid = (deferred: Deferred.Deferred<void>) => {
      Effect.runSync(Deferred.succeed(deferred, void 0).pipe(Effect.ignore))
    }

        sessionID,
        agent: "build",
        model: ref,
        time: { created: Date.now() },
      })

    it.instance("loop surfaces content-filter finishes as session errors", () =>
      Effect.gen(function* () {
        const { llm } = yield* useServerConfig(providerCfg)

        const prompt = yield* SessionPrompt.Service

        const errors: NonNullable<SessionV1.Assistant["error"]>[] = []
        const expected = {
          name: "ContentFilterError",
          data: { message: "The response was blocked by the provider's content filter" },
        } satisfies NonNullable<SessionV1.Assistant["error"]>
        const off = yield* events.listen((event) => {
          if (event.type !== Session.Event.Error.type) return Effect.void
          const data = event.data as typeof Session.Event.Error.data.Type
          if (data.sessionID === chat.id && data.error) errors.push(data.error)
          return Effect.void
        })
    
        yield* prompt.prompt({
          sessionID: chat.id,
          agent: "build",

        }
        expect(result.parts).toEqual(
          expect.arrayContaining([expect.objectContaining({ type: "text", text: "partial response" })]),
        )
      }),
    )

    import { $ } from "bun"
    import { ConfigV1 } from "@opencode-ai/core/v1/config/config"

    import { InstanceStore } from "../../src/project/instance-store"
    import { TestLLMServer } from "../lib/llm-server"
    
    const noopBootstrap = Layer.succeed(InstanceBootstrap.Service, InstanceBootstrap.Service.of({ run: Effect.void }))
    export const testInstanceStoreLayer = LayerNode.compile(InstanceStore.node, [
      [InstanceStore.bootstrapNode, noopBootstrap],
    ])
    
    export async function provideTestInstance<R>(input: {
      directory: string
      init?: Effect.Effect<void>
      fn: (ctx: InstanceContext) => R
    }) {
      const ctx = await InstanceRuntime.load({ directory: input.directory })
      try {
        if (input.init) await Effect.runPromise(input.init.pipe(Effect.provideService(InstanceRef, ctx)))
        return await input.fn(ctx)
      } finally {
        await InstanceRuntime.disposeInstance(ctx)
      }
    }
    
    export async function withTestInstance<R>(input: { directory: string; fn: (ctx: InstanceContext) => R }) {
      return input.fn(await InstanceRuntime.load({ directory: input.directory }))
    }
    
    export async function reloadTestInstance(input: { directory: string }) {
      return InstanceRuntime.reloadInstance(input)
    }
    
    export async function disposeAllInstances() {
      await InstanceRuntime.disposeAllInstances()
    }
    
    // Strip null bytes from paths (defensive fix for CI environment issues)
    function sanitizePath(p: string): string {
      return p.replace(/\0/g, "")
    }
    
    function exists(dir: string) {
      return fs
        .stat(dir)
        .then(() => true)
        .catch(() => false)
    }
    
    function clean(dir: string) {
      return fs.rm(dir, {
        recursive: true,
        force: true,
        maxRetries: 5,
        retryDelay: 100,
      })
    }
    
    async function stop(dir: string) {
      if (!(await exists(dir))) return
      await $`git fsmonitor--daemon stop`.cwd(dir).quiet().nothrow()
    }
    
    type TmpDirOptions<T> = {
      git?: boolean
      config?: Partial<ConfigV1.Info>
      init?: (dir: string) => Promise<T>
      dispose?: (dir: string) => Promise<T>
    }
    export async function tmpdir<T>(options?: TmpDirOptions<T>) {
      const dirpath = sanitizePath(path.join(os.tmpdir(), "opencode-test-" + Math.random().toString(36).slice(2)))
      await fs.mkdir(dirpath, { recursive: true })
      if (options?.git) {
        await $`git init`.cwd(dirpath).quiet()
        await $`git config core.fsmonitor false`.cwd(dirpath).quiet()
        await $`git config commit.gpgsign false`.cwd(dirpath).quiet()
        await $`git config user.email "test@opencode.test"`.cwd(dirpath).quiet()
        await $`git config user.name "Test"`.cwd(dirpath).quiet()
        await $`git commit --allow-empty -m "root commit ${dirpath}"`.cwd(dirpath).quiet()
      }
      if (options?.config) {
        await Bun.write(
          path.join(dirpath, "opencode.json"),
          JSON.stringify({
            $schema: "https://opencode.ai/config.json",
            ...options.config,
          }),
        )
      }
      const realpath = sanitizePath(await fs.realpath(dirpath))
      const extra = await options?.init?.(realpath)
      const result = {
        [Symbol.asyncDispose]: async () => {
          try {
            await options?.dispose?.(realpath)
          } finally {
            if (options?.git) await stop(realpath).catch(() => undefined)
            await clean(realpath).catch(() => undefined)
          }
        },
        path: realpath,
        extra: extra as T,
      }
      return result
    }
    
    /** Effectful scoped tmpdir. Cleaned up when the scope closes. Make sure these stay in sync */
    export function tmpdirScoped<E = never, R = never>(options?: {
      git?: boolean
      config?: Partial<ConfigV1.Info> | (() => Partial<ConfigV1.Info>)
      init?: (directory: string) => Effect.Effect<void, E, R>
    }) {
      return Effect.gen(function* () {
        const spawner = yield* ChildProcessSpawner.ChildProcessSpawner
        const dirpath = sanitizePath(path.join(os.tmpdir(), "opencode-test-" + Math.random().toString(36).slice(2)))
        yield* Effect.promise(() => fs.mkdir(dirpath, { recursive: true }))
        const dir = sanitizePath(yield* Effect.promise(() => fs.realpath(dirpath)))
    
        yield* Effect.addFinalizer(() =>
          Effect.promise(async () => {
            if (options?.git) await stop(dir).catch(() => undefined)
            await clean(dir).catch(() => undefined)
          }),
        )
    
        const git = (...args: string[]) =>
          spawner.spawn(ChildProcess.make("git", args, { cwd: dir })).pipe(Effect.flatMap((handle) => handle.exitCode))
    
        if (options?.git) {
          yield* git("init")
          yield* git("config", "core.fsmonitor", "false")
          yield* git("config", "commit.gpgsign", "false")
          yield* git("config", "user.email", "test@opencode.test")
          yield* git("config", "user.name", "Test")
          yield* git("commit", "--allow-empty", "-m", `root commit ${dir}`)
        }
    
        if (options?.config) {
          const resolved = typeof options.config === "function" ? options.config() : options.config
          yield* Effect.promise(() =>
            fs.writeFile(
              path.join(dir, "opencode.json"),
              JSON.stringify({ $schema: "https://opencode.ai/config.json", ...resolved }),
            ),
          )
        }
    
        if (options?.init) yield* options.init(dir)
    
        return dir
      })
    }
    
    export const provideInstance =
      (directory: string) =>
      <A, E, R>(self: Effect.Effect<A, E, R>): Effect.Effect<A, E, R | InstanceStore.Service> =>
        InstanceStore.Service.use((store) => store.provide({ directory }, self))
    
    export const provideInstanceEffect =
      (directory: string) =>
      <A, E, R>(self: Effect.Effect<A, E, R>): Effect.Effect<A, E, R | InstanceStore.Service> =>
        InstanceStore.Service.use((store) => store.provide({ directory }, self))
    
    export const reloadInstance = (input: InstanceStore.LoadInput) =>
      InstanceStore.Service.use((store) => store.reload(input))
    
    export const disposeAllInstancesEffect = InstanceStore.Service.use((store) => store.disposeAll())
    
    export function provideTmpdirInstance<A, E, R>(
      self: (path: string) => Effect.Effect<A, E, R>,
      options?: { git?: boolean; config?: Partial<ConfigV1.Info> | (() => Partial<ConfigV1.Info>) },
    ) {
      return Effect.gen(function* () {
        const path = yield* tmpdirScoped(options)
        return yield* self(path).pipe(provideInstance(path))
      }).pipe(Effect.provide(testInstanceStoreLayer))
    }
    
    export class TestInstance extends Context.Service<TestInstance, { readonly directory: string }>()("@test/Instance") {}
    
    export const requireInstance = Effect.gen(function* () {
      const instance = yield* InstanceRef
      if (!instance) return yield* Effect.die(new Error("missing test instance"))
      return instance
    })
    
    export const withTmpdirInstance =
      <E2 = never, R2 = never>(options?: {
        git?: boolean
        config?: Partial<ConfigV1.Info> | (() => Partial<ConfigV1.Info>)
        init?: (directory: string) => Effect.Effect<void, E2, R2>
      }) =>
      <A, E, R>(self: Effect.Effect<A, E, R>) =>
        Effect.gen(function* () {
          const directory = yield* tmpdirScoped(options)
          return yield* self.pipe(Effect.provideService(TestInstance, { directory }), provideInstanceEffect(directory))
        }).pipe(Effect.provide(testInstanceStoreLayer), Effect.provide(AppNodeBuilder.build(CrossSpawnSpawner.node)))
    
    export function provideTmpdirServer<A, E, R>(
      self: (input: { dir: string; llm: TestLLMServer["Service"] }) => Effect.Effect<A, E, R>,
      options?: { git?: boolean; config?: (url: string) => Partial<ConfigV1.Info> },
    ): Effect.Effect<
      A,
      E | PlatformError.PlatformError,
      R | TestLLMServer | ChildProcessSpawner.ChildProcessSpawner | Scope.Scope
    > {

## Assistant

The `/associate` suite currently has 5 tests: 3 pass and 2 fail.

Key finding: the two “valid file” tests create files inside the temporary instance directory, but the non-Git fixture resolves `ctx.worktree` to `/`. Production validation therefore looks for `/src/...`, not `<temp-dir>/src/...`.

Affected tests:

- [prompt.test.ts:1819](/workspaces/fall-26-opencode-team-zero/packages/opencode/test/session/prompt.test.ts:1819)
- [prompt.test.ts:1840](/workspaces/fall-26-opencode-team-zero/packages/opencode/test/session/prompt.test.ts:1840)

Validation responsible:

- [prompt.ts:1381](/workspaces/fall-26-opencode-team-zero/packages/opencode/src/session/prompt.ts:1381)

Focused test result:

```text
3 pass
2 fail
```

The straightforward correction is to run the valid-path cases in Git-backed fixtures (`{ git: true }`), making the temporary repository the project root. The missing-file test should also use a Git fixture; although it currently passes, it does not prove lookup happens relative to the intended project root.

Coverage is otherwise reasonable for:

- Normal relative path expansion
- Quoted paths containing spaces
- Missing arguments
- Multiple-argument rejection
- Missing-file rejection
- Ensuring invalid input does not call the LLM

Useful missing cases include:

- Absolute file paths
- Directory paths being rejected
- Existing files resolved specifically from the project root
- Error-event publication
- Unmatched or malformed quoting

One additional observation: the quoted-path test asserts that quotes remain in `$ARGUMENTS`, while validation strips them. That is internally consistent, but it should be an intentional contract.

No files were changed.

The implementation is not isolated to the slash-command prompt: `Command.Info` is also the response schema for the public command-list HTTP endpoint and ACP command discovery. That makes the new metadata (`maxArguments`, `fileArgument`) externally visible, so I’m checking whether generated API artifacts and downstream command adapters reflect it, in addition to type-checking the package.

## Assistant

## `/associate` overview

`/associate` is a built-in slash command intended to map every callable in one source file to the tests that exercise it.

Its behavior is split between:

- Command registration and metadata: [command/index.ts:93](/workspaces/fall-26-opencode-team-zero/packages/opencode/src/command/index.ts:93)
- Model instructions: [associate.txt](/workspaces/fall-26-opencode-team-zero/packages/opencode/src/command/template/associate.txt:1)
- Argument and file validation: [session/prompt.ts:1372](/workspaces/fall-26-opencode-team-zero/packages/opencode/src/session/prompt.ts:1372)
- Tests: [prompt.test.ts:1819](/workspaces/fall-26-opencode-team-zero/packages/opencode/test/session/prompt.test.ts:1819)

## Intended behavior

The command accepts:

```text
/associate path/to/source.ts
/associate "path containing spaces/source.ts"
/associate
```

It rejects more than one parsed argument:

```text
/associate first.ts second.ts
```

With the staged changes, it also rejects a supplied path that is not an existing file.

When no path is provided, execution is allowed to reach the model. The prompt tells the model to ask the user for a path and stop.

The requested report is:

| Function | Source | Test(s) | Association |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Callable name | File and line | Test references | `direct`, `indirect`, or `untested` |

The prompt also requests a coverage-gaps section when untested functions exist and explicitly prohibits editing or generating tests without a later request.

## Execution flow

1. `associate` is registered as a built-in command.
2. Its description is exposed through command discovery.
3. The raw argument string is tokenized using:

   ```ts
   /(?:\[Image\s+\d+\]|"[^"]*"|'[^']*'|[^\s"']+)/gi
   ```

4. Outer quotes are removed for validation.
5. `maxArguments: 1` rejects multiple tokens.
6. With the staged implementation, `fileArgument: true` resolves relative paths against `ctx.worktree` and checks `fsys.isFile`.
7. The original raw argument string—including surrounding quotes—is substituted into `$ARGUMENTS`.
8. The expanded instructions are sent through the normal session/model path.
9. The model performs the actual source-to-test analysis.

Only input validation is implemented in code. Identifying functions, finding tests, and classifying associations are entirely model-driven.

## Current repository state

The branch is:

```text
npeters/associate-tests
```

Relevant commits:

- `b43c75a` — creates `/associate`
- `119216c` — adds maximum-argument validation and four tests

There are also staged, uncommitted changes in:

- `packages/opencode/src/command/index.ts`
- `packages/opencode/src/session/prompt.ts`
- `packages/opencode/test/session/prompt.test.ts`

Those staged changes add:

- `fileArgument` command metadata
- Runtime file-existence validation
- A missing-file test
- File setup for the two successful-path tests

The configured `dev` and `origin/dev` references are absent in this checkout. The available upstream base is `origin/main`.

## Test coverage

Five `/associate` tests currently exist.

| Test | Purpose | Current result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Expands a relative path | Verifies template expansion and model invocation | Fails |
| Accepts a quoted path | Verifies a path containing spaces is one argument | Fails |
| Accepts no path | Verifies the missing-path instruction reaches the model | Passes |
| Rejects multiple paths | Verifies argument limit and zero model calls | Passes |
| Rejects missing file | Verifies file validation and zero model calls | Passes |

Focused execution result:

```text
3 pass
2 fail
58 filtered out
```

Package type-check result:

```text
bun typecheck
$ tsgo --noEmit
```

The type-check passes.

## Primary test defect

The successful-path tests create files under the temporary instance directory:

- [prompt.test.ts:1821](/workspaces/fall-26-opencode-team-zero/packages/opencode/test/session/prompt.test.ts:1821)
- [prompt.test.ts:1842](/workspaces/fall-26-opencode-team-zero/packages/opencode/test/session/prompt.test.ts:1842)

But these tests use non-Git temporary fixtures. For a non-VCS project, project resolution assigns `/` as `ctx.worktree`. Validation consequently searches for:

```text
/src/session/prompt.ts
/src/session/prompt with spaces.ts
```

rather than files inside the temporary directory.

The tests should use Git-backed fixtures so the temporary repository becomes the worktree. The missing-file test should do the same; it currently passes without proving that resolution uses the correct project root.

## Coverage limitations

The current suite tests command plumbing, not the principal user-facing feature. It does not establish that the model:

- Reads the complete source file
- Finds relevant test files
- Identifies all callable definitions
- Distinguishes direct from indirect coverage
- Avoids associations based only on matching names
- Includes correct line references
- Reports untested functions
- Produces the required Markdown table
- Avoids editing files

Those behaviors are prompt instructions and remain nondeterministic and untested.

Additional parser and validation cases worth covering:

- Absolute paths
- Existing directory rejection
- Resolution from the project root when the current directory differs
- Single-quoted paths
- Empty quoted arguments such as `""`
- Unmatched quotes
- Escaped quotes inside paths
- Whitespace-only input
- Symlinks
- Files outside the project
- Files that exist but cannot be read
- Publication of `Session.Event.Error`

## Public API issue

`Command.Info` is used as the public response schema for the command-list HTTP endpoint:

- [instance.ts:138](/workspaces/fall-26-opencode-team-zero/packages/opencode/src/server/routes/instance/httpapi/groups/instance.ts:138)

The changes add these public fields:

```ts
maxArguments?: number
fileArgument?: boolean
```

However, the checked-in OpenAPI definition still lacks both fields:

- [openapi.json:22475](/workspaces/fall-26-opencode-team-zero/packages/sdk/openapi.json:22475)

The generated SDK and client types are therefore stale relative to the runtime API. Under the repository instructions, the client/API generation process should be run rather than editing generated files manually.

There is also a design question: these fields currently appear to be internal execution metadata, but placing them in `Command.Info` exposes them publicly. If clients are expected to use them for input UX, regeneration is correct. If they are internal-only, they should be separated from the public command schema.

ACP exposes the command’s name and description but discards the new metadata when advertising available commands:

- [acp/service.ts:948](/workspaces/fall-26-opencode-team-zero/packages/opencode/src/acp/service.ts:948)

Thus ACP clients cannot use `maxArguments` or `fileArgument` for client-side guidance.

## Behavioral concerns

### “Readable” is not actually validated

The prompt says the path must identify a readable file. Runtime validation only calls `fsys.isFile`, which establishes that it is a file, not necessarily that it can be read.

### Paths outside the project are accepted

Absolute paths are explicitly accepted. Relative paths containing `..` can also escape the project root after `path.resolve`.

This may be intentional, but it conflicts with a strict interpretation of “resolve relative paths from the project root” and broadens access beyond the project. Symlinks can have the same effect.

### Quoted input has two representations

Validation removes surrounding quotes, but `$ARGUMENTS` substitution preserves the raw input:

```text
Input file: "src/file with spaces.ts"
```

This is internally workable, but it is a contract worth documenting and testing intentionally.

### Empty argument handling is delegated to the model

No argument is not rejected by application code. Instead, the model is instructed to ask for a file.

The test only checks that the instruction text was sent. It does not prove that the assistant asks the user or stops without further analysis.

### Argument parsing is limited

The tokenizer supports simple single- and double-quoted strings, but not escaped quotes or shell-grade parsing. Malformed quotes may be partially ignored and interpreted as a different path rather than producing a syntax error.

### Custom configuration can replace the built-in

Configured commands are inserted after built-ins. A user-defined command named `associate` replaces the built-in record and does not inherit `maxArguments` or `fileArgument`. This is consistent with complete override semantics, but it means `/associate` validation is not guaranteed when that name is customized.

## Prompt quality

The prompt is concise and has strong guardrails:

- It separates direct and indirect associations.
- It warns against relying on name matches.
- It requires source and test line references.
- It explicitly reports untested functions.
- It prevents unsolicited test generation or edits.

Potential ambiguities remain:

- “Every function” versus selectively including anonymous callbacks
- How overloaded functions, constructors, accessors, class fields, and nested functions should be represented
- Whether framework-driven tests count as direct or indirect
- Whether setup hooks and fixtures should be listed
- How to report parameterized tests
- Whether type declarations and abstract methods should be excluded
- How to handle generated or platform-specific tests
- What “complete source file” means for very large files

## Overall assessment

The command concept and prompt are coherent, and generic maximum-argument validation is cleanly integrated. Type-checking passes.

It is not currently ready as a fully verified change because:

1. Two of five focused tests fail.
2. The successful-path fixture setup uses the wrong project-root model.
3. Generated public API artifacts are stale.
4. The central association behavior has no behavioral tests.
5. File validation checks existence/type, not actual readability.
6. Project-boundary behavior is unspecified and currently permits external paths.

## User

how would you fix the current implementation of the /associate command?

## Assistant

I would fix it in this order:

1. Correct the test fixtures

Run every path-validation test in a Git-backed temporary project:

```ts
it.instance(
  "associate command accepts an existing source file",
  () =>
    Effect.gen(function* () {
      const { dir, llm } = yield* useServerConfig(providerCfg)
      yield* writeText(path.join(dir, "src/session/prompt.ts"), "export function prompt() {}")
      const { prompt, chat } = yield* boot()
      yield* llm.text("done")

      const result = yield* prompt.command({
        sessionID: chat.id,
        command: "associate",
        arguments: "src/session/prompt.ts",
      })

      expect(result.info.role).toBe("assistant")
      expect(yield* llm.calls).toBe(1)
    }),
  { git: true },
)
```

Apply `{ git: true }` to:

- Existing relative path
- Existing quoted path
- Missing path
- Ideally directory and absolute-path cases

This aligns the fixture directory with `ctx.worktree`.

2. Make file validation a clear command-level concept

The current `fileArgument` Boolean is vague. I would use explicit metadata such as:

```ts
argument: Schema.optional(
  Schema.Struct({
    maximum: Schema.optional(Schema.Int.check(Schema.isGreaterThanOrEqualTo(0))),
    type: Schema.optional(Schema.Literals(["file"])),
  }),
)
```

However, if only `/associate` needs this behavior, the smaller `maxArguments` and `fileArgument` implementation is acceptable. Avoid expanding the abstraction unless another command needs it.

3. Separate internal metadata from the public schema

`Command.Info` is returned by the command-list HTTP endpoint, so `fileArgument` and `maxArguments` unintentionally become public API fields.

I would choose one of two explicit designs:

- Public metadata: keep the fields, run API generation, and let clients use them for input validation.
- Internal metadata: create an internal command record extending the public `Command.Info`, and omit execution-only fields from HTTP responses.

I prefer public `maxArguments`, because clients can present better errors. `fileArgument` may be better expressed publicly as an argument specification rather than a Boolean.

4. Centralize validation without over-extracting it

The command function should retain a simple happy path:

```ts
const raw = input.arguments.match(argsRegex) ?? []
const args = raw.map((arg) => arg.replace(quoteTrimRegex, ""))

if (cmd.maxArguments !== undefined && args.length > cmd.maxArguments) {
  return yield* failCommand(...)
}

if (cmd.fileArgument && args[0]) {
  const ctx = yield* InstanceState.context
  const filepath = path.isAbsolute(args[0]) ? args[0] : path.resolve(ctx.worktree, args[0])

  if (!(yield* fsys.isFile(filepath))) {
    return yield* failCommand(...)
  }
}
```

A small reusable error helper would be justified because both branches construct, publish, and throw the same error type. Otherwise, the current inline structure is reasonable.

5. Decide whether paths may escape the project

Currently these are accepted:

```text
/associate /etc/example.ts
/associate ../../outside-project.ts
```

I would restrict the command to files inside `ctx.worktree`, unless external files are an explicit requirement. Resolve and normalize the path, then verify it remains under the project root. Real-path resolution should also be considered so symlinks cannot silently escape the project.

If external paths are intentionally supported, document and test that behavior.

6. Validate readability, not just file type

The template promises a “readable file,” but `isFile` only checks the path type. Either:

- Change the wording to “existing file,” or
- Actually attempt a read/access check before invoking the model.

The implementation and prompt should make the same promise.

7. Improve argument parsing edge cases

Add tests for:

- Single-quoted paths
- Whitespace-only arguments
- Empty quotes: `""`
- Unterminated quotes
- Existing directory
- Absolute path
- `../` traversal
- Symlinks
- Unicode and special characters

Malformed quotes should produce a syntax error instead of being partially discarded by the regex.

8. Strengthen assertions

Successful tests should verify:

```ts
expect(yield* llm.calls).toBe(1)
```

Failure tests should verify:

```ts
expect(yield* llm.calls).toBe(0)
```

They should also listen for `Session.Event.Error` and confirm the published error matches the thrown error.

9. Test prompt behavior separately

The current tests prove that prompt text is forwarded, but not that `/associate` produces a correct association report.

I would add a stable integration test with a deterministic test model response or a prompt snapshot that verifies the required instructions:

- Complete source inspection
- Direct/indirect/untested classifications
- Line-number references
- Markdown table
- No file editing

The model’s semantic analysis itself should not be asserted through brittle natural-language output.

10. Regenerate public artifacts

If the new fields remain in `Command.Info`, run the repository’s API generation workflow and commit the resulting OpenAPI/client/SDK updates. Generated files should not be edited manually.

The immediate minimal fix is: add `{ git: true }` to the path tests, rerun them, and regenerate API artifacts if the metadata stays public. The more complete fix also defines project-boundary and readability semantics explicitly.

    export function hints(template: string) {
      const result: string[] = []
      const numbered = template.match(/\$\d+/g)
      if (numbered) {
        for (const match of [...new Set(numbered)].sort()) result.push(match)
      }
      if (template.includes("$ARGUMENTS")) result.push("$ARGUMENTS")
      return result
    }

    export namespace FSUtil {
      export class FileSystemError extends Schema.TaggedErrorClass<FileSystemError>()("FileSystemError", {
        method: Schema.String,
        cause: Schema.optional(Schema.Defect()),
      }) {
        override get message() {
          const detail = this.cause instanceof Error ? this.cause.message : this.cause && String(this.cause)
          return `Filesystem operation failed: ${this.method}${detail ? `: ${detail}` : ""}`
        }
      }
    
      export type Error = PlatformError | FileSystemError
    
      export interface DirEntry {
        readonly name: string
        readonly type: "file" | "directory" | "symlink" | "other"
      }
    
      export interface Interface extends FileSystem.FileSystem {
        readonly isDir: (path: string) => Effect.Effect<boolean>
        readonly isFile: (path: string) => Effect.Effect<boolean>

        readonly readJson: (path: string) => Effect.Effect<unknown, Error>

        readonly ensureDir: (path: string) => Effect.Effect<void, Error>
        readonly writeWithDirs: (path: string, content: string | Uint8Array, mode?: number) => Effect.Effect<void, Error>
        readonly readDirectoryEntries: (path: string) => Effect.Effect<DirEntry[], Error>
        readonly resolve: (path: string) => Effect.Effect<string>
        readonly findUp: (target: string, start: string, stop?: string) => Effect.Effect<string[], Error>

        readonly glob: (pattern: string, options?: Glob.Options) => Effect.Effect<string[], Error>
        readonly globMatch: (pattern: string, filepath: string) => boolean
      }
    
      export class Service extends Context.Service<Service, Interface>()("@opencode/FileSystem") {}
    
      export const use = serviceUse(Service)

## Assistant

The path checks now canonicalize both the project root and supplied file, reject traversal/symlink escapes, require a readable regular file, and accept absolute paths only when they remain inside the project. The tests now use Git-backed roots and cover relative, quoted, absolute, missing, directory, and outside-project paths. I’m running the focused suite now.

## Assistant

The focused behavior is now green. One cleanup remains from the broader review: `maxArguments` and `fileArgument` are execution-only data but currently alter the public `Command` HTTP schema. I’m separating their internal type from the public schema so no SDK regeneration is needed for private validation details.

## Assistant

Implemented the `/associate` path-handling improvements.

Changes include:

- Git-backed test fixtures so relative paths resolve against the correct project root.
- Acceptance tests for relative, quoted, and in-project absolute paths.
- Rejection tests for missing files, directories, and paths outside the project.
- Canonical path resolution, including protection against symlink/path traversal escapes.
- Readability validation before invoking the model.
- `maxArguments` and `fileArgument` moved to internal runtime metadata, avoiding an unintended public API/schema change.
- Successful cases now assert exactly one LLM call; rejected cases assert zero.

## User

how else would you test the /associate command?

## Assistant

I’d add coverage in four areas.

### Argument parsing

- Single-quoted paths: `'src/file with spaces.ts'`
- Whitespace-only input
- Empty quoted arguments: `""`
- Unterminated quotes
- Leading and trailing whitespace
- Tabs and newlines between arguments
- Unicode filenames
- Filenames containing quote-like or shell-special characters

Malformed quoting should ideally produce a clear syntax error rather than becoming a surprising path.

### Path validation

- Symlink inside the project targeting:
  - A file inside the project — accept
  - A file outside the project — reject
- Normalized paths such as `src/../src/file.ts`
- Absolute outside-project paths
- Unreadable regular files
- Broken symlinks
- FIFO/socket/device paths, if supported by the test platform
- Project-root file such as `README.md`
- Nested worktrees or repository subdirectories
- Windows path behavior, especially drive letters and separators

Some permission and symlink tests should be platform-gated.

### Side effects and errors

The existing rejection tests check the thrown error and LLM call count. I would also verify:

- Exactly one `Session.Event.Error` is published
- The published error equals the thrown error
- No user or assistant message is persisted on validation failure
- No command-executed event is emitted for rejected input
- A successful command emits the expected execution event
- File validation occurs before model/provider resolution

A table-driven test could cover most validation failures without duplicating the same `Effect.exit` assertions.

### Prompt contract

Tests should confirm the expanded prompt contains all essential instructions:

- The correct path
- Complete-file inspection
- Direct versus indirect distinction
- `untested` classification
- Source and test line references
- Required Markdown columns
- Coverage-gaps behavior
- The prohibition against editing or generating tests

I would use a prompt snapshot or explicit checks for stable semantic clauses. Testing exact full wording would make harmless prompt edits unnecessarily expensive.

### Command discovery

Test that `/associate`:

- Appears exactly once in `Command.list()`
- Has the expected description and `$ARGUMENTS` hint
- Is returned by the HTTP command-list endpoint
- Is advertised through ACP
- Can be deliberately overridden by a configured command named `associate`
- Does not expose internal `maxArguments` or `fileArgument` metadata publicly

### End-to-end association behavior

Create a small fixture project containing:

- A directly tested exported function
- A helper reached indirectly through the exported function
- An untested function
- A misleading same-named test that does not execute the function

Then run `/associate` with a deterministic model/test harness and verify the resulting report classifies them as:

```text
direct
indirect
untested
untested—not merely matched by name
```

This is the most valuable behavioral test, but it should avoid asserting exact prose. Parse or inspect table rows and references instead.

### Highest-priority additions

If keeping the suite compact, I would add these first:

1. Symlink escape rejection.
2. Normalized valid path acceptance.
3. Malformed quote rejection.
4. Error-event publication with no persisted prompt.
5. Command-list/public-schema coverage.
6. A fixture containing direct, indirect, and untested functions.

## User

why does it reject paths outside the project?

## Assistant

It rejects outside-project paths because I added that restriction as a security boundary based on the earlier review.

Without it, these inputs could cause the agent to inspect arbitrary files available to the process:

```text
/associate /etc/passwd
/associate ../../other-project/private.ts
```

Canonicalizing both paths also prevents a symlink inside the project from silently pointing outside it.

However, this was a design choice—not an explicit requirement in the original `/associate` prompt. The prompt only says:

> Resolve relative paths from the project root.

That does not necessarily mean absolute or parent-relative external paths must be rejected. If OpenCode normally permits users to reference files outside the project, this restriction may be too aggressive.

The two reasonable policies are:

- Project-scoped: safer and predictable; reject anything whose canonical path escapes the project.
- Filesystem-scoped: accept any readable file the OpenCode process can access; more flexible and closer to the prompt’s literal wording.

Given the existing requirement only says “a single file path,” I would remove the project-boundary restriction unless project isolation is an established OpenCode policy. I’d retain canonical path resolution, regular-file validation, and readability validation.

## User

Opencode is meant to be a tool to help code other projects would this cause problems with that purpose?

## Assistant

Not for the normal case: when OpenCode is launched inside the project being developed, that project becomes `ctx.worktree`, so `/associate src/file.ts` works correctly.

But the restriction could cause legitimate problems when users need to inspect:

- Files in a neighboring repository
- Shared source outside the repository root
- Monorepo dependencies linked from another directory
- External worktrees
- System-installed library source
- Symlinked packages whose targets live outside the project
- Files supplied by an editor or client using absolute paths

OpenCode already has a broader permission system governing filesystem access. Adding a separate project-root restriction specifically to `/associate` creates inconsistent behavior: the agent might be allowed to read a file normally, yet `/associate` rejects it before the permission system can evaluate access.

So yes, it could work against OpenCode’s general-purpose coding role. I recommend removing this check:

```ts
const relative = path.relative(root, filepath)
if (relative.startsWith(`..${path.sep}`) || relative === ".." || path.isAbsolute(relative)) {
  // reject
}
```

Keep these protections instead:

- Resolve relative paths from `ctx.worktree`
- Canonicalize the supplied path
- Require exactly one regular file
- Require it to be readable
- Let the existing filesystem permission system control external-file access

The outside-project rejection test should then become an acceptance test using an absolute external temporary file—or be removed if the fixture cannot reliably represent the normal permission flow.

## User

describe all the tests for /associate

## Assistant

There are currently nine focused `/associate` tests in [prompt.test.ts](/workspaces/fall-26-opencode-team-zero/packages/opencode/test/session/prompt.test.ts).

1. `associate command expands the source file argument`

Creates `src/session/prompt.ts` inside a temporary Git project and invokes:

```text
/associate src/session/prompt.ts
```

It verifies that:

- The command completes with an assistant response.
- The `/associate` instructions reach the model.
- The relative path is inserted into `Input file:`.
- The LLM is called exactly once.

2. `associate command accepts one quoted source file argument`

Creates a file whose name contains spaces and invokes:

```text
/associate "src/session/prompt with spaces.ts"
```

It verifies that:

- The quoted path is parsed as one argument.
- The command completes successfully.
- The original quoted argument appears in the expanded prompt.
- The LLM is called exactly once.

3. `associate command accepts no source file argument`

Invokes `/associate` with an empty argument string.

It verifies that:

- The command is allowed to reach the model.
- The prompt tells the model to ask the user for a file and stop.
- An assistant response is produced.

It does not verify that the model actually asks the question; it only verifies that the instruction is present.

4. `associate command rejects multiple source file arguments`

Invokes:

```text
/associate src/foo.ts src/bar.ts
```

It verifies that:

- Command execution fails.
- The failure is a `NamedError.Unknown`.
- The message says `/associate` accepts at most one argument.
- The LLM is never called.

5. `associate command rejects a source file that does not exist`

Invokes `/associate` with a nonexistent relative path inside a Git-backed temporary project.

It verifies that:

- Command execution fails.
- The error identifies the missing path.
- The LLM is never called.

6. `associate command accepts an absolute source file path inside the project`

Creates a source file and invokes `/associate` using its absolute path.

It verifies that:

- An absolute path is accepted when it identifies a valid file.
- The absolute path reaches the expanded prompt.
- The command produces an assistant response.
- The LLM is called exactly once.

7. `associate command rejects paths outside the project`

Invokes:

```text
/associate ../outside.ts
```

It verifies that:

- Canonical path validation detects that the path escapes the project.
- The error says the file must be inside the project.
- The LLM is never called.

As discussed, this test reflects a policy that may be too restrictive for OpenCode. If external files should be supported, this test and the corresponding implementation should be changed.

8. `associate command rejects directories`

Invokes:

```text
/associate .
```

Because `.` identifies the project directory rather than a regular file, it verifies that:

- The command rejects directories.
- The failure uses the current `File not found` message.
- The LLM is never called.

The behavior is correct, although a message such as “Path is not a file” would be more precise.

9. `associate command rejects an unreadable source file`

This Unix-only test creates a real file and removes all of its permissions with `chmod(file, 0)`.

It verifies that:

- An existing but unreadable regular file is rejected.
- The error says `File is not readable`.
- The LLM is never called.
- The test is skipped on Windows, where Unix permission semantics do not apply.

Together, these tests currently provide 100% line coverage of the `/associate` argument and file-validation block: 34 of 34 executable lines. They cover command plumbing and path validation, but not the quality of the model-generated function-to-test association report.

## User

how would you test the output does what it is supposed to?

## Assistant

I would test the output at three levels: prompt contract, deterministic fixture evaluation, and optional real-model evaluation.

### 1. Prompt-contract test

Verify that the expanded prompt contains every required instruction:

- Inspect the complete source file.
- Identify named functions, methods, and callable exports.
- Search for tests using repository conventions and references.
- Distinguish `direct`, `indirect`, and `untested`.
- Include file paths and line numbers.
- Produce the required four-column Markdown table.
- Add coverage gaps only when needed.
- Do not modify files.

This catches accidental prompt regressions, but does not prove the model follows the instructions.

### 2. Deterministic fixture project

Create a tiny Git-backed project with deliberately unambiguous relationships:

```text
src/math.ts
test/math.test.ts
test/misleading.test.ts
```

For example:

```ts
// src/math.ts
export function add(a: number, b: number) {
  return normalize(a) + normalize(b)
}

function normalize(value: number) {
  return Number(value)
}

export function subtract(a: number, b: number) {
  return a - b
}
```

```ts
// test/math.test.ts
import { add } from "../src/math"

test("adds values", () => {
  expect(add(1, 2)).toBe(3)
})
```

```ts
// test/misleading.test.ts
test("subtract", () => {
  expect("subtract").toContain("subtract")
})
```

The expected associations are:

| Function | Expected |
| --- | --- |
| `add` | `direct` |
| `normalize` | `indirect` through `add` |
| `subtract` | `untested` |
| Misleading name match | Must not count as coverage |

This fixture exercises the most important reasoning requirements.

### 3. Parse output semantically

Avoid comparing the entire response as a snapshot. Natural-language wording can vary without changing correctness.

Parse the Markdown table and assert:

```ts
expect(rows).toEqual(
  expect.arrayContaining([
    expect.objectContaining({
      function: "add",
      association: expect.stringContaining("direct"),
    }),
    expect.objectContaining({
      function: "normalize",
      association: expect.stringContaining("indirect"),
    }),
    expect.objectContaining({
      function: "subtract",
      association: expect.stringContaining("untested"),
    }),
  ]),
)
```

Also assert:

- Exactly one row exists for each expected callable.
- Referenced paths exist.
- Line numbers are valid and point near the named symbol or test.
- `subtract` is not associated with `misleading.test.ts`.
- The coverage-gaps section mentions `subtract`.
- No repository files changed during execution.

### The model-test limitation

The existing test LLM returns queued text such as `"done"`. It cannot inspect the fixture and generate a genuine association report. Therefore, a normal unit test cannot prove the feature’s reasoning quality.

There are two viable approaches.

#### Recorded deterministic response

Queue a representative Markdown response and test only that the command transports and stores it correctly.

This tests session behavior, but it does not test association reasoning.

#### Real-model evaluation

Run `/associate` against the fixture using a real supported model, then grade the output semantically.

The evaluator should calculate:

- Function recall: Were all expected functions included?
- Association accuracy: Were direct, indirect, and untested labels correct?
- False-positive rate: Did name-only matches get rejected?
- Reference accuracy: Do paths and line numbers exist?
- Format compliance: Is the required table present?
- Safety compliance: Were no files modified?

For example:

```text
Function recall:       3/3
Classification:        3/3
False-positive checks: 1/1
Valid references:      4/4
Required format:       pass
No mutations:          pass
```

Because model output can vary, this should be an evaluation suite or opt-in integration test rather than a required fast unit test.

### Strongest long-term design

For reliably testable output, have `/associate` request structured data first:

```ts
{
  associations: [
    {
      function: "add",
      source: { path: "src/math.ts", line: 1 },
      tests: [{ path: "test/math.test.ts", line: 3 }],
      association: "direct",
      evidence: "Called directly by the test"
    }
  ],
  gaps: ["subtract"]
}
```

Then render the Markdown table in application code. That makes schema validation, classification checks, path validation, deduplication, and Markdown formatting deterministic. The model still performs the analysis, but the output contract becomes substantially easier to evaluate.
